                             II
                                            Abstract



                                         Abstract
     X-ray computed tomography (CT) is an advanced non-invasive imaging technique that
enables three-dimensional visualization of the internal structure of the scanned object. Due to
its fast scanning speed and high-resolution imaging, CT has become a primary medical
imaging modality. It is widely used for disease screening, diagnosis, and efficacy assessment,
as well as providing image guidance in scenarios such as interventional procedures and
radiotherapy. However, radiation exposure in CT may lead to genetic damage and induce
cancers in a probability. The well-known ALARA (as low as reasonably achievable) principle
has been proposed to prevent radiation abuse. Theoretically, the radiation dose can be reduced
by lowering the x-ray tube current, decreasing the measured views, or narrowing the scan
range. However, the measured data acquired under the above non-ideal configurations are
severely degraded. Moreover, some specific clinical applications inevitably result in the
acquired measurement data being sparse or incomplete. In these cases, the directly
reconstructed CT images suffer from severe noise and artifacts and thus cannot provide
accurate image information for the follow-up diagnostic or therapeutic procedures. Achieving
high-quality CT reconstruction under non-ideal scanning configurations is a hot topic of great
scientific and clinical significance.
     In addition, dual-energy CT (DECT) has advantages over conventional CT in identifying
different materials, which makes it unique in clinical practice. However, current DECT
imaging heavily relies on dedicated systems such as the dual-source scheme from Siemens in
Germany, the fast kV switching scheme from General Electric in the US, and the dual-layer
detector scheme from Philips in the Netherlands. Updating proprietary devices for DECT
requires expensive hardware costs, hindering more patients from benefiting from DECT
imaging. What鈥檚 more, as DECT gains widespread acceptance in the diagnostic radiology
community, the interest in applying DECT technology to improve applications such as
interventional procedures and radiotherapy is also increasing. However, the related devices
have not yet integrated the dual-energy imaging technique. Hence, developing new DECT
imaging schemes compatible with existing devices is of great clinical significance.
     In this context, this study utilizes the advanced deep learning technique in the field of
computer science to design appropriate imaging algorithms for specific clinical applications,
aiming to achieve high-quality CT reconstruction under non-ideal sampling configurations
and provide accurate image information for subsequent diagnostic or therapeutic procedures.
Details of this study are provided below.
     (1) Dual-Domain Generator based Adversarial Reconstruction for Low-Dose CT. In
clinical practice, the radiation dose is usually reduced by lowering the x-ray tube current,
which will result in severe noise in the directly reconstructed images. Existing algorithms that
work only in the projection domain or only in the image domain may lead to secondary
artifacts or subtle structural distortions because they ignore the inherent correlation between
the projection data and image data. To address this problem, this study proposes the
dual-domain generator based adversarial reconstruction for low-dose CT. Different from
                                               III
                                     涓滃崡澶у鍗氬＋瀛︿綅璁烘枃

generators built in the data domain or image domain, the dual-domain generator network help
to surpass the limitations of the data space and can extract richer information. The adopted
3D model can also exploit correlations between neighbouring slices to enhance the
algorithm鈥檚 performance in detail restoration. The designed dual-domain loss function can
impose supervision at the different reconstruction stages to encourage all the generator
components to be updated toward high-quality reconstruction. The introduced adversarial
learning mechanism encourages the generator to produce results consistent with high-quality
CT images regarding statistical distribution to avoid the blurring effect. Experimental results
on simulated abdominal data and real phantom data have demonstrated the promising
performance of the proposed method in terms of noise suppression, tiny structure restoration,
and visual perception enhancement.
     (2) Dual Deep Regularizers Enhanced Iterative Reconstruction for Sparse-View CT. For
applications such as 3D angiography that require long exposure times per projection angle,
sparse-view scanning can significantly reduce the accumulated radiation dose and acquisition
time. To reconstruct high-quality images for sparse-view CT, this study takes into account the
potential of projection inpainting in improving data consistency and proposes the dual deep
regularizers enhanced iterative reconstruction algorithm. Different from the conventional
unrolled iterative reconstruction algorithms that only perform constraints in the image domain,
the proposed method imposes constraints in the projection domain, residual domain, and
image domain through three cascaded modules (i.e., projection inpainting, residual awareness,
and image enhancement), improving data consistency, residual accuracy, and image fidelity
simultaneously. The hyperparameters involved in the iterative algorithm for balancing
different terms are implicitly included in network modules and do not require empirical
adjustment or explicit learning. The base networks utilize efficient and lightweight
encoder-decoder architecture rather than several cascaded convolutional layers, which helps
achieve promising performance using fewer iterations. Experimental results on simulated
abdominal data and real mouse data show that the proposed method can gradually improve
image quality in a progressive improvement manner and outperform related methods in
artifact removal and detail restoration.
     (3) Dual-Domain Learning Enhanced Reconstruction for Sparse-View DECT. The fast
kV switching system for DECT is highly dependent on sophisticated and costly hardware and
suffers from the difficulty of tube current modulation. Taking into account the data
correlation between low- and high-energy channels in DECT, this study proposes a
complementary sparse scanning scheme based on the slow kV switching technique and
develops the dual-domain learning enhanced reconstruction algorithm for sparse-view DECT.
The scanning scheme has the potential to reduce the technical cost, and it can acquire the
information about the scanned object as much as possible and reduce the x-ray exposure dose.
Meanwhile, this study develops the dual domain joint learning enhanced reconstruction
algorithm for sparse-view DECT, which simultaneously performs projection inpainting in the
data domain and residual artifact removal in the image domain. Both the projection domain
sub-network and the image domain sub-network adopt the dual-stream ResUNet designed for
DECT, in which the shared encoder can fully exploit the complementary information between
                                              IV
                                           Abstract

low- and high-energy data while the low-energy and high-energy encoders can effectively
perform differential learning for dual-energy data. Experiments on the simulated abdominal
data under three different sparse-view configurations demonstrate that the proposed method
has promising performance in streaking artifact removal and fine feature restoration.
     (4) Dual-Domain Prior Enhanced Deep Iterative Reconstruction for Limited-Angle
DECT. With the widespread acceptance of DECT in the diagnostic radiology community, the
interest in using DECT technology to improve applications such as interventional procedures
and radiotherapy is increasing. However, the related devices do not integrate dual-energy
imaging. Considering that the limited-angle scan is usually used to acquire data in these
scenarios, this study proposes a DECT acquisition scheme based on dual-quarter scans. This
scheme enables the acquisition of dual-energy data in a limited angular range and can reduce
the x-ray exposure dose and shorten the scan time. To reconstruct high-quality images from
incomplete measurement data, this study proposes the dual-domain prior enhanced deep
iterative reconstruction algorithm for limited-angle DECT. Based on the traditional prior
constrained iterative reconstruction framework, this algorithm further introduces the
projection prior and image prior to improve the performance in projection inpainting and
image restoration. Furthermore, the dense connections fusing information from different
iterations can effectively avoid gradient vanishing and stabilize the model performance.
Finally, the deep iterative reconstruction network is embedded as a generator in the
adversarial learning framework to enhance the visual appearance of the generated DECT
images. Experimental results on simulated abdominal data and real mouse data demonstrate
that the proposed method performs better than the competing methods regarding wedge
artifact removal, HU value correction, and image detail recovery.


Keywords: Computed tomography, deep learning, dual-domain processing, deep iterative
reconstruction, generative adversarial networks, prior constrained
